# Research: Auth Bounded Context

## Technology Decisions

### Better Auth Integration

- **Decision**: Use Better Auth with Drizzle adapter for email/password authentication
- **Rationale**: Constitution mandates Better Auth (technology stack). Drizzle adapter provides native PostgreSQL integration without additional ORM mapping.
- **Implementation**: Initialize Better Auth in `shared/auth.ts` with `better-auth` + drizzle adapter. Configure email/password provider, fixed session TTL (7d, no refresh), and rate limiting.
- **Alternatives considered**: Lucia (deprecated), next-auth (React-focused, Nuxt integration less mature)

### Session Strategy

- **Decision**: Cookie-based sessions with httpOnly, Secure, SameSite=Lax
- **Rationale**: Better Auth natively supports cookie sessions. Nuxt/Nitro handles cookie serialization. No custom JWT management needed. **SameSite=Lax** (não `Strict`) para permitir links mágicos por email (verificação, reset de senha) funcionarem cross-site — o navegador envia o cookie no GET que segue o link.
- **Session validation**: Middleware (`server/middleware/auth.ts`) reads session cookie, calls Better Auth `getSession()`, sets `event.context.user`.
- **Concurrent sessions**: FR-012 allows multiple active sessions per user (different devices).

### Rate Limiting

- **Decision**: Sliding window rate limit (5 req/min per email) with in-memory counter or Drizzle-based tracking
- **Rationale**: Sliding window prevents burst resets (unlike fixed window). Tracked per email address for precision. FR-010 specifies 429 response format with Retry-After, RateLimit-Reset, RateLimit-Remaining headers.
- **Implementation**: Better Auth provides built-in rate limiting or use Nitro's `defineEventHandler` wrapper for custom implementation.

### Error Response Format

- **Decision**: Consistent JSON error body across all endpoints
- **Rationale**: FR-005 (CHK005) mandates consistent error format. FR-010 (429), FR-024/FR-025 (503), FR-026 (500) define slot-specific formats. All follow `{ "error": "snake_case_code", "message": "Human-readable message" }` pattern.
- **Edge case**: 401 for expired mid-request sessions returns `{ "error": "session_expired", "message": "Session expired. Please login again." }`.

### CORS Configuration

- **Decision**: Explicit allowed origin (e.g., `https://app.merp.com.br`) with `Access-Control-Allow-Credentials: true`
- **Rationale**: Cookie-based auth requires explicit origin (wildcard `*` + credentials is invalid per spec). Preflight `OPTIONS` requests handled by Nitro CORS configuration.

### Timeouts

- **Decision**: 5s timeout for Better Auth library calls, 10s for OAuth provider calls
- **Rationale**: Balances against existing p95 targets (SC-001: 2s, SC-002: 1s). Prevents cascading failures without excessive false positives.

### Initialization Failure Handling

- **Decision**: Graceful degradation — return 503 from auth endpoints + health check, log error, keep process alive
- **Rationale**: Process crash prevents ops from accessing health endpoints. 503 allows load balancers to route traffic away.


===== FILE: specs/001-auth-context/spec.md =====

specs/001-auth-context/spec.md: Unicode text, UTF-8 text

# Feature Specification: Auth Bounded Context

**Feature Branch**: `001-auth-context`

**Created**: 2026-06-11

**Status**: Draft

**Input**: User description: "Build the Auth bounded context for Merp."

## Overview

The Auth bounded context handles user identity — registration, login,
logout, and session management. It is a supporting domain that every
other context depends on for authenticated access and multi-tenant
company membership.

**Why this exists**: An ERP manages sensitive fiscal and financial data.
Without authentication, there is no way to know who is accessing the
system, which tenant they belong to, or whether they are authorized.
Auth is the gate — every interaction flows through it.

## Clarifications

### Session 2026-06-11

- Q: What are the session expiry TTL values? → A: Fixed 7d TTL from
  creation (`expiresIn: 7d`, `disableSessionRefresh: true` — no sliding
  refresh). `rememberMe: true` persists the cookie for 7d; without it the
  cookie is session-scoped. The DB row always expires 7d after creation.
- Q: How are companies created and users assigned to them? → A:
  Self-service: after registration, the user can create a new company
  (becoming its first member) or search for an existing company to join.
- Q: Should auth operations be logged for audit purposes? → A: Yes —
  all auth events (login, failed attempt, registration, logout) are
  logged.
- Q: What is the account lockout policy? → A: Lock after 10 total
  failed attempts (any time window), auto-unlock after 30 minutes.
- Q: What is the user lifecycle — can users be deactivated or deleted?
  → A: Soft delete: users are flagged as inactive, never hard-deleted.
- Q: When a session expires mid-request (e.g., during a multi-step
  operation like fiscal note emission), what should the system do?
  → A: Return HTTP 401 with
  `{ "error": "session_expired", "message": "Session expired. Please login again." }`
  and include a `session_expired` error code.
- Q: Should rate-limit responses include a `RateLimit-Remaining` header?
  → A: Yes, include `RateLimit-Remaining` in addition to `Retry-After`
  and `RateLimit-Reset`.
- Q: What timeouts should be configured for external calls? → A: 5s
  timeout for Better Auth / session operations, 10s timeout for OAuth
  provider HTTP calls.
- Q: What should happen when shared/auth.ts initialization fails at
  startup? → A: Return HTTP 503 from auth endpoints and health check,
  log the error, keep the process alive (graceful degradation).
- Q: Should registration have explicit idempotency key support to
  prevent duplicate accounts on network retry? → A: No — email
  uniqueness (FR-003) already prevents duplicates. Same-email retries
  return the same generic `201 Created` as a new registration
  (anti-enumeration, AUD-03) — never `409 Conflict`.
- Q: What should happen when an unexpected 500 error occurs in an
  auth endpoint? → A: Return 500 with
  `{ "error": "internal_error", "message": "An unexpected error occurred." }`,
  log full details server-side, never leak stack traces.
- Q: What CORS configuration should auth endpoints use? → A: Allow
  specific production origin (e.g., `https://app.merp.com.br`) with
  credentials (`Access-Control-Allow-Credentials: true`). No wildcard
  origins — cookies require explicit origin.

## User Scenarios & Testing _(mandatory)_

### User Story 1 - Email & Password Registration and Login (Priority: P1)

A new user registers with email and password, then logs in with those
credentials. After login, the system maintains an active session across
requests.

**Why this priority**: Email/password auth is the baseline for any
multi-tenant system. Every feature — fiscal note emission, financial
operations, tenant management — requires an authenticated user.
Without this story, nothing else works.

**Independent Test**: Register with email+password, then login with the
same credentials, then access a protected resource successfully.

**Acceptance Scenarios**:

1. **Given** a new email and password, **When** the user registers,
   **Then** the system creates an account and returns a success response.
2. **Given** an existing account, **When** the user logs in with correct
   credentials, **Then** the system starts an authenticated session.
3. **Given** an existing account, **When** the user logs in with an
   incorrect password, **Then** the system denies access with an error.
4. **Given** an email already registered, **When** another user tries to register
   with the same email, **Then** the system responds 201 Created with body
   identical to a new registration (AUD-03, anti-enumeration) and does not
   create a duplicate (FR-003 = uniqueness in DB, not error code). Never 409.

---

### User Story 2 - Logout and Session Lifecycle (Priority: P2)

An authenticated user can end their session explicitly. Sessions expire
automatically after a period of inactivity.

**Why this priority**: Users must be able to securely end access to the
system. Sessions that never expire are a security risk in a fiscal
environment. P2 because login (P1) must exist first to have something
to log out from.

**Independent Test**: Login, receive a session, logout, then attempt an
authenticated request and receive an access-denied error.

**Acceptance Scenarios**:

1. **Given** an active session, **When** the user logs out,
   **Then** the session is terminated.
2. **Given** no active session, **When** the user accesses a protected
   resource, **Then** the system denies access.
3. **Given** an active session past its 7d TTL, **When** the user
   makes a request, **Then** the system denies access.

---

### User Story 3 - Multi-Tenant Membership (Priority: P3)

After registration, the user can create a new company (becoming its
first member) or **join exclusively via invite**. Once a member of at
least one company, the user operates within that tenant context.

**Why this priority**: Merp serves multiple companies. User identity
without tenant context is incomplete — we need to know _which company_
the user is acting on behalf of. P3 because tenant membership builds on
top of basic auth (P1) and session management (P2).

**Independent Test**: Register, create a company, login, then verify
authenticated requests carry the correct tenant context.

**Acceptance Scenarios**:

1. **Given** a registered user with no company, **When** the user
   creates a new company, **Then** they become its first member.
2. **Given** a registered user, **When** they accept a valid invite
   token for a company, **Then** they become a member.
3. **Given** a user with one company, **When** the user logs in,
   **Then** the system defaults to their company context.
4. **Given** a user member of multiple companies, **When** the user
   logs in, **Then** they can switch between companies.
5. **Given** a user not assigned to any company, **When** they log in,
   **Then** the system returns a success but limited-access status.

---

### Edge Cases

- What happens when a user registers with an already-used email?
- What happens when the session expires mid-operation (e.g., during
  fiscal note emission)? → The system returns HTTP 401 with
  `{ "error": "session_expired", "message": "Session expired. Please login again." }`.
  The client retries after re-authentication. A `session_expired`
  observability event is emitted per FR-023.
- How does the system handle concurrent sessions from different devices? → Allowed per FR-012. No limit enforced.
- What happens when the identity store is unreachable during login? → The system returns HTTP 503 with `{ "error": "service_unavailable", "message": "Temporary error. Please try again." }` per FR-028.
- What happens when repeated failed login attempts occur? → Login attempts from a given IP are locked for 30 min after 10 failed attempts per FR-016; after 50 total failed attempts across IPs the account is locked for everyone for 30 min (AUD-02).
- How does the system behave under high registration volume? → Register requests are rate-limited per FR-010.
- What happens when a deactivated user tries to log in? → Login denied with HTTP 401 per FR-017.
- What happens to a user's sessions when their account is deactivated? → All sessions immediately invalidated per FR-017.

## Requirements _(mandatory)_

### Functional Requirements

- **FR-001**: System MUST allow user registration with email and password.
- **FR-002**: System MUST validate email format and enforce a minimum
  password length during registration.
- **FR-003**: System MUST reject registration with a duplicate email.
- **FR-004**: System MUST allow login with email and password.
- **FR-005**: System MUST maintain an authenticated session after
  successful login, valid across subsequent requests.
- **FR-006**: System MUST allow logout by terminating the current session.
- **FR-007**: System MUST reject unauthenticated requests to protected
  resources.
- **FR-008**: System MUST protect stored passwords against compromise
  (e.g., hashing).
- **FR-009**: Sessions MUST expire 7d after creation (fixed TTL:
  `expiresIn: 7d` with `disableSessionRefresh: true` — no sliding refresh).
  The `rememberMe` flag controls cookie persistence only (7d persistent vs
  session-scoped cookie); the server-side row always expires 7d after creation.
  **Regra operacional atual (PDV/KDS compartilhado)**: TTL 7d + logout
  obrigatório ao fim do turno. Evolução planejada em ADR-004 (TTL por perfil
  - PIN de turno).
- **FR-010**: Login attempts MUST be rate-limited to mitigate brute-force
  attacks. Rate limiting uses a **sliding window** of 5 requests per 1 minute per
  email address. When the limit is exceeded, the server
  responds with HTTP 429 Too Many Requests. The response body MUST follow
  the standard error format:
  `{ "error": "rate_limit_exceeded", "message": "Too many requests. Try again later.", "retryAfter": <seconds> }`.
  Response headers MUST include `Retry-After` (seconds until reset),
  `RateLimit-Reset` (Unix timestamp of window expiry), and
  `RateLimit-Remaining` (remaining requests in current window).
- **FR-011**: System MUST associate each authenticated user with at least
  one company (tenant) for authorization decisions.
- **FR-013**: Registration MUST create an account only. Company setup
  is a separate step after registration.
- **FR-014**: System MUST provide an endpoint to create a new company,
  making the requesting user its first member.
- **FR-015**: System MUST provide an endpoint to accept an invite
  token and join a company (join exclusivamente via convite).
- **FR-016**: System MUST lock login attempts after 10 failed attempts
  from the same IP for an email (any time window); locked (email, IP) pairs
  auto-unlock after 30 minutes. Additionally, the system MUST lock the account
  for all IPs after 50 total failed attempts across IPs (distributed brute
  force with IP rotation, AUD-02), also auto-unlocking after 30 minutes.
- **FR-017**: System MUST support deactivating a user (soft delete).
  Deactivated users cannot log in, but their data is preserved.
- **FR-018**: System MUST log all auth events — registration, login,
  failed login, and logout — with timestamp, email, and outcome.
- **FR-012**: System MUST support multiple active sessions per user
  (different devices/browsers).
- **FR-019**: System MUST support company deletion. Only company owners
  can delete (no platform `super_admin` role exists — deferred). Deletion is a soft-delete (`is_deleted`
  flag) to preserve referential integrity for fiscal records. Cascade
  behavior: all CompanyMember rows purged, all pending invites purged,
  company no longer selectable as tenant context. Active sessions for
  the user's other tenants remain valid.
- **FR-020**: System MUST provide a data export endpoint returning all
  user personal data (profile, memberships, sessions, auth events) in JSON
  format, fulfilling LGPD data portability rights (Art. 18). Only events
  linked to the requesting user are included.
- **FR-021**: System MUST record user consent at registration
  (acceptance of terms and data processing) with timestamp and version.
  Consent withdrawal MUST trigger immediate account deactivation and
  session termination. An automated PII deletion/anonymization queue is
  deferred (out of scope) — withdrawal is recorded as a
  `consent_withdrawal` audit event.
- **FR-022**: System MUST log all access to PII data (email, name) in
  the audit log — who accessed it, when, and for what purpose. PII
  access logs are retained for 5 years.
- **FR-023**: System MUST emit structured observability events for
  session lifecycle: session_created, session_validated,
  session_expired, session_invalidated. Each event includes session_id,
  user_id, tenant_id (if applicable), and timestamp.
- **FR-024**: System MUST enforce HTTP timeouts for all outbound calls:
  5s for Better Auth library calls, 10s for OAuth provider calls.
  Timeout failures MUST return HTTP 503 with
  `{ "error": "upstream_timeout", "message": "External service timed out. Try again." }`
  and MUST be logged with full error details.
- **FR-025**: If `shared/auth.ts` initialization fails at startup
  (e.g., missing env vars, Better Auth config error), the system MUST
  NOT crash. Instead, auth endpoints and health check MUST return
  HTTP 503 with
  `{ "error": "auth_unavailable", "message": "Authentication service unavailable." }`.
  The initialization error MUST be logged with full details.
- **FR-026**: All unexpected 500-level errors MUST return a consistent
  response body:
  `{ "error": "internal_error", "message": "An unexpected error occurred." }`.
  Stack traces, internal paths, and implementation details MUST NOT be
  leaked in the response. The full error MUST be logged server-side
  with correlation ID for debugging.
- **FR-027**: CORS MUST be configured with explicit allowed origins
  (no wildcard). Auth endpoints MUST include
  `Access-Control-Allow-Credentials: true` for session cookie support.
  Preflight (`OPTIONS`) requests MUST respond with appropriate CORS
  headers for the configured origins.
- **FR-028**: Request body validation MUST use **Zod** schemas named per
  endpoint (ex.: `createOrderSchema`, `loginSchema`). Validation errors
  return 400 with `details` per field (consistent with
  `specs/000-foundation/endpoint-pattern.md`).
- **FR-028**: If the database is unreachable during any auth operation,
  the system MUST return HTTP 503 with
  `{ "error": "service_unavailable", "message": "Temporary error. Please try again." }`.
  The underlying database error MUST NOT be leaked. The event MUST be
  logged with full error details.
- **FR-029**: System MUST verify email ownership at registration and
  block login until verification (AUD-04). Registration MUST send a
  verification link; login with correct credentials on an unverified
  account MUST return 403 `email_not_verified` (reachable only with the
  correct password — never leaks account existence). Verification links
  expire after 1h; replay is idempotent. The system MUST provide a
  resend endpoint (`send-verification-email`) whose response is
  indistinguishable for unknown/already-verified accounts.
- **FR-030**: System MUST provide a password recovery flow ("forgot
  password") (AUD-04): `forget-password` sends a single-use reset link
  (expires 1h) to the account owner's email and always answers
  `{ "status": true }` (anti-enumeration); `reset-password` consumes the
  token (replay MUST fail with 400) and revokes ALL sessions on
  success. Recovery MUST work for any account with a password —
  including accounts pre-registered by a third party — so the real
  email owner can always take the account back.

### Key Entities

- **User**: A person who can access the system. Has an email, can
  authenticate, and has an active/inactive state. Belongs to one or
  more companies.
- **Session**: Represents an authenticated user's active access period.
  Created on login, destroyed on logout or expiry.
- **Company**: A tenant in the multi-tenant model. Users are members of
  companies and operate within their scope.

## Success Criteria _(mandatory)_

### Measurable Outcomes

- **SC-001**: Registration completes in under 2 seconds (p95).
- **SC-002**: Login completes in under 1 second (p95).
- **SC-003**: Session validation (authenticated request overhead) is
  under 100ms (p95).
- **SC-004**: Duplicate registration returns an error in under 500ms.
- **SC-005**: Unauthenticated requests return an access-denied response
  in under 50ms.
- **SC-006**: a 6ª requisição no window de 60s (por email) retorna 429 com
  header `Retry-After`. Limites completos em `specs/000-foundation/endpoint-pattern.md`.

## Assumptions

- Email delivery (verification, password reset, welcome emails) is out
  of scope for the MVP.
- User profile management (name, avatar, preferences) is out of scope
  for the MVP.
- Multi-tenancy scoping (which company a user belongs to) is managed
  at the auth layer — downstream contexts consume the tenant context
  from the authenticated request.
- OAuth / social login is deferred — email+password is sufficient for
  the initial launch.
- Role-based access control (admin, operator, viewer) is deferred —
  the MVP treats all authenticated users within a company as having
  equal access.
- The auth context does not handle fiscal rules, financial transactions,
  or any domain logic outside identity and access.

## Threat Model & Security Scope

**In scope for MVP**:

- Brute-force mitigation via rate limiting (sliding window, 5 req/min per
  email) and account lockout (10 failures, 30 min auto-unlock).
- Session token protection: httpOnly, Secure, SameSite=Lax cookies.
  Tokens never exposed in URLs, logs, or error messages.
- Credential protection: passwords hashed by Better Auth (bcrypt, cost
  factor 10+). Passwords never logged or returned in responses.
- Multi-tenancy isolation: RLS at database layer + withTenant at
  application layer. Defense in depth prevents cross-tenant data leaks.
- TLS enforcement assumed at reverse proxy layer (not part of this
  context's implementation).

**Out of scope for MVP**:

- OAuth / social login flows and their CSRF protections.
- Role-based access control (RBAC) — all authenticated members have equal
  access within a tenant.
- API key management and programmatic access tokens.
- Audit trail for non-auth events (data access, mutations) — deferred to
  each bounded context.
- Secrets rotation, certificate management, and infrastructure-level
  security hardening (WAF, DDoS protection).
- Penetration testing and formal security audit — deferred to pre-launch
  hardening phase.

**Monitoring & alerting thresholds**:

| Metric                    | Warning                  | Critical        | Alert                 |
| ------------------------- | ------------------------ | --------------- | --------------------- |
| Login failure rate        | >20% of attempts in 5min | >50% in 5min    | Ops email + dashboard |
| Rate-limit hits/min       | >100 hits/min            | >500 hits/min   | Ops email + SMS       |
| Registration rate         | >50/min                  | >200/min        | Ops email             |
| Session validation errors | >10% in 5min             | >30% in 5min    | Ops email             |
| PII access (anomalous)    | >2x normal rate          | >5x normal rate | Security team email   |

All thresholds are configurable via environment variables. Metrics are
exposed via a `/metrics` endpoint (Prometheus format) for dashboard
integration.

**Security review trigger**: A formal security review MUST be conducted
before any production deployment that serves real user data. The review
must cover: session management implementation, rate limiting correctness,
RLS policy validation, and error message leakage.

## Data Governance & Compliance

### PII Classification

The following fields contain Personally Identifiable Information (PII)
under Brazilian LGPD:

| Field           | Classification      | Sensitivity | Retention                                      |
| --------------- | ------------------- | ----------- | ---------------------------------------------- |
| email           | Direct identifier   | High        | Indefinite while active, 5y after deactivation |
| name            | Direct identifier   | Medium      | Same as email                                  |
| hashed_password | Auth secret         | High        | Same as email (irreversibly hashed)            |
| CNPJ            | Indirect identifier | Medium      | Indefinite (fiscal record)                     |
| razao_social    | Business data       | Low         | Indefinite (fiscal record)                     |
| IP address      | Behavioral          | Low         | 90 days (auth logs)                            |
| session_token   | Auth secret         | High        | 24h / 7d (session TTL)                         |

### Data Retention & Purging

- **Sessions**: Expired sessions (past TTL + 24h grace) purged daily.
  Remember-me sessions purged 48h after 7d TTL.
- **Auth logs**: Retained for 90 days, then aggregated (counts only)
  and purged.
- **Expired invites**: Purged 30 days after `expiresAt`.
- **Deactivated accounts**: Data retained for 5 years (fiscal compliance
  with Brazilian archival law), then permanently anonymized — email and
  name stripped, hashed_password destroyed, IP addresses in logs
  truncated to /24.
- **Hard-deletion on demand**: Upon verified user request, PII fields
  (email, name) are overwritten with `"[deleted]"` placeholders.
  Sessions and memberships are immediately removed. Auth logs and fiscal
  foreign-key references are preserved but disassociated from the
  deleted identity.

### Data Breach Notification

Per LGPD Art. 48:

1. **Detect**: Monitor auth failure rate anomalies (>500% above
   baseline) and unauthorized access attempts. Alert ops within 5
   minutes.
2. **Assess**: Determine scope (data categories, affected user count,
   records exposed) within 2 hours of detection.
3. **Notify ANPD**: Within 24h with breach description, affected data
   categories, user count, and containment measures.
4. **Notify affected users**: Within 48h via email with breach
   description, data involved, and recommended actions (password change,
   account monitoring).

### Cross-Border Data Transfers

| Service           | Data Transited    | Jurisdiction    | Safeguard                                    |
| ----------------- | ----------------- | --------------- | -------------------------------------------- |
| PostgreSQL        | All user data     | USA (us-east-1) | SCC + encryption at rest (AES-256) + TLS 1.3 |
| Better Auth (lib) | In-memory hashing | N/A (local lib) | No transfer                                  |
| Resend (email)    | Email, name       | USA             | SCC + Data Processing Agreement              |

Per LGPD Art. 33, adequate safeguards (Standard Contractual Clauses +
DPA) are maintained with each provider.

## Infrastructure Dependencies

The `withTenant(tenantId, fn)` helper is the mandatory entry point for
all queries involving tenant-scoped data within this bounded context:

- **Member queries** (reading/writing company memberships) MUST use
  `withTenant`.
- **Company queries** (reading company details, searching) MUST use
  `withTenant`.
- **Invite and permission data** (future scope) MUST use `withTenant`.
- Direct `db` access is permitted ONLY for company creation (onboarding)
  and global lookup tables — the initial `INSERT INTO companies` is the
  sole exception.

**Testing note**: Integration tests MUST verify that queries executed
outside `withTenant` return zero rows for tenant-scoped tables (RLS
deny by default). This validates that the RLS policy is correctly
enforcing isolation even when the application layer is bypassed.

**Concurrency testing**: The invite acceptance flow MUST be tested for
race conditions. Two simultaneous requests accepting the same invite
MUST result in exactly one membership created (the second request
fails with 409 Conflict). Tests MUST use PostgreSQL's
`SERIALIZABLE` isolation level or explicit locks to verify correct
behavior under concurrent access. The repository layer MUST use
row-level locking (`SELECT ... FOR UPDATE`) when reading invite status
before creating the membership.

## Dependencies & Execution Order

### Phase Dependencies

- **Setup (Phase 1)**: No dependencies — can start immediately
- **Foundational (Phase 2)**: Depends on Setup — BLOCKS all user stories
- **US1 — Registration & Login (Phase 3)**: Depends on Foundational — No dependencies on other stories
- **US2 — Logout & Session (Phase 4)**: Depends on Foundational — May reuse US1 components (session model), independently testable
- **US3 — Multi-Tenant (Phase 5)**: Depends on Foundational + US1 — Requires login (US1) to test tenant context
- **Polish (Phase 6)**: Depends on all user stories

### User Story Dependencies

- **US1 (P1)**: Can start after Foundational — No dependencies on other stories
- **US2 (P2)**: Can start after Foundational — Reuses session from US1 but independently testable
- **US3 (P3)**: Depends on US1 (login required) — Independently testable once US1 is complete

### Parallel Opportunities

- All tasks marked **[P]** in any phase can run in parallel (different files, no dependencies)
- US1 and US2 can be worked on in parallel once Foundational is done
- US3 must wait for US1 to be complete

## Implementation Strategy

### MVP First (User Story 1 Only)

1. Complete Phase 1: Setup
2. Complete Phase 2: Foundational (CRITICAL — blocks all stories)
3. Complete Phase 3: User Story 1
4. **STOP and VALIDATE**: Test User Story 1 independently
5. Deploy/demo if ready

### Incremental Delivery

1. Complete Setup + Foundational → Foundation ready
2. Add User Story 1 → Test independently → Deploy/Demo (MVP!)
3. Add User Story 2 → Test independently → Deploy/Demo
4. Add User Story 3 → Test independently → Deploy/Demo
5. Each story adds value without breaking previous stories

### Parallel Team Strategy

With multiple developers:

1. Team completes Setup + Foundational together
2. Once Foundational is done:
   - Developer A: User Story 1
   - Developer B: User Story 2
3. After US1 completes:
   - Developer C: User Story 3
4. Stories complete and integrate independently

===== FILE: specs/002-vertical-slices/architecture.md =====

specs/002-vertical-slices/architecture.md: Unicode text, UTF-8 text, with very long lines (536)

# Reestruturação para Vertical Slices (Package by Feature)

**Status**: Implementado (fases 1–3 e 4[middleware] concluídas; fases 0, 5 e 6 parciais) | **Data**: 2026-08-06 | **Repo**: merp

> Objetivo: reorganizar o código do contexto `auth` (e servir de modelo para
> `fiscal` e `financeiro`) de uma organização **por camada técnica** para uma
> organização **por feature** — cada feature é um "pacote" vertical com seu
> próprio repositório, tipos (DTOs), helpers e testes, no espírito do
> _package by feature_ comum em Go.

---

## 1. Diagnóstico da Estrutura Atual

### 1.1 Inventário

```
server/
├── api/
│   ├── health.get.ts                        # 23 linhas
│   └── auth/                                # 18 handlers (35–88 linhas cada)
│       ├── register.post.ts  login.post.ts  logout.post.ts  me.get.ts
│       ├── deactivate.post.ts  export.get.ts
│       ├── companies.post.ts  companies.get.ts  companies.switch.post.ts
│       ├── companies.update.post.ts  companies.delete.post.ts
│       ├── companies/members.delete.post.ts
│       ├── consent/withdraw.post.ts
│       ├── invite.post.ts  invites.get.ts  invites.accept.post.ts
├── middleware/
│   ├── auth.ts                              # validação de sessão + tenant ctx (97)
│   └── pii-audit.ts                         # auditoria de acesso a PII (26)
├── tasks/
│   └── purge-sessions.ts                    # limpeza diária de sessões
├── error-handler.ts                         # normalização global de erros
└── utils/
    ├── schema.ts                            # 198 linhas — TODAS as tabelas
    ├── db.ts                                # conexão (infra)
    ├── apply-custom-sql.ts                  # infra (migrações RLS)
    ├── test-setup.ts                        # infra (setup de testes)
    └── auth/
        ├── repository.ts                    # 881 linhas — GOD REPOSITORY
        ├── helpers.ts                       # 301 linhas — MISTURA DE CONCERNAS
        └── tenant-context.ts                # withTenant (infra)
shared/
├── auth.ts                                  # instância Better Auth
└── logger.ts                                # logger
```

### 1.2 Problemas Identificados

| #   | Problema                                                                                                                                                                                         | Evidência                                                     |
| --- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------- |
| P1  | **God repository**: um único `IAuthRepository` com 30+ métodos cobre 5 subdomínios distintos (identidade, sessão, empresa/tenant, convite, consentimento/auditoria)                              | `repository.ts` — 881 linhas                                  |
| P2  | **Coesão inexistente**: não existe o conceito de "feature" no código — handlers de assuntos diferentes apontam todos para o mesmo repositório e helpers                                          | todos os handlers importam `authRepository`                   |
| P3  | **Helpers misturam concernas**: contrato de erro HTTP, validação, rate limiting, lockout e timeouts vivem no mesmo arquivo                                                                       | `helpers.ts` — 301 linhas                                     |
| P4  | **Schema único mistura domínios**: tabelas de auth puro (user/session/account) convivem com tenant (company/company_member), convite (invite) e segurança (auth_security_state) no mesmo arquivo | `schema.ts` — 198 linhas                                      |
| P5  | **Risco de acoplamento futuro**: quando `fiscal` (core domain) crescer, o padrão atual forçará novos `IXxxRepository` globais em `server/utils/{context}/` — replicando o mesmo problema         | constituição §II prevê `server/utils/{context}/repository.ts` |
| P6  | **Interface gigante viola ISP**: qualquer handler depende de métodos de todos os subdomínios; mocks de teste precisam stubbear 30+ métodos                                                       | `repository.spec.ts` / mocks nos handlers                     |
| P7  | **Tabelas compartilhadas sem dono claro**: `auth_event` (auditoria) e `auth_security_state` (segurança) não têm "feature" dona                                                                   | usados por middleware, repository e handlers                  |

### 1.3 O que JÁ está bom (não mudar)

- Handlers finos e delgados (validar → chamar → responder) — padrão correto.
- Co-locação de testes (`.spec.ts` / `.test.ts`) junto ao código.
- `withTenant` como único ponto de entrada de contexto de tenant.
- Contrato de erro global (`error-handler.ts`) e helpers `sendXxxError`.
- Nomes de arquivo por rota (`register.post.ts` etc.).

---

## 2. Modelo Alvo: Package by Feature (estilo Go)

### 2.1 Princípios

1. **Uma feature = um diretório vertical**: contém tudo o que a feature
   precisa — repositório, DTOs, helpers, testes. Nada de importar a feature
   do vizinho.
2. **API pública explícita**: cada feature exporta apenas o necessário
   (ex.: `identity/repository.ts` exporta `identityRepository`, a classe
   `IdentityRepository` e os DTOs/interfaces). O resto
   fica privado ao diretório.
3. **Sem "god objects"**: se um repositório tem mais de ~8–10 métodos ou
   toca mais de 3 tabelas, é sinal de que há mais de uma feature ali dentro.
4. **Fronteiras por domínio de negócio**, não por camada técnica.
5. **Infra compartilhada explícita**: `db.ts`, `tenant-context.ts`,
   `schema registry`, contrato de erros ficam num diretório `shared` do
   servidor — a única exceção à regra da verticalidade.

### 2.2 Restrições do Nuxt/Nitro que moldam o design

| Restrição                                                      | Consequência de design                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| -------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **URL da rota = caminho do arquivo** em `server/api/`          | Handlers NÃO podem ser aninhados por feature sem mudar a URL. Ex.: `server/api/auth/identity/register.post.ts` viraria `/api/auth/identity/register`. Logo, os handlers continuam em `server/api/auth/` (flat) — a verticalidade se expressa no **núcleo** (`server/utils/auth/<feature>/`). |
| **Auto-imports** (`nitro.imports.dirs`)                        | Hoje importa `server/utils/auth` inteiro. Após a divisão, apontar para os arquivos certos ou manter imports explícitos (o código já usa imports explícitos via `~~`).                                                                                                                        |
| **Drizzle exige um ponto de entrada de schema** para migrações | `drizzle.config.ts` aponta para `./server/utils/schema.ts`. O schema pode ser dividido por feature **desde que** exista um registry raiz re-exportando tudo (única fonte para `drizzle-kit` e para o adapter do Better Auth).                                                                |
| **Middleware é global**                                        | `server/middleware/auth.ts` é cross-cutting — permanece onde está, mas passa a usar os repositórios das features (não o god repository).                                                                                                                                                     |

---

## 3. Estrutura Alvo

### 3.1 Árvore completa

```text
server/
├── api/
│   ├── health.get.ts                          # público (infra)
│   ├── metrics.get.ts                         # público, Prometheus sem PII
│   ├── .well-known/security.txt.get.ts        # público (RFC 9116)
│   └── auth/                                  # ← ROTAS: permanecem flat (21 handlers)
│       ├── register.post.ts  login.post.ts  logout.post.ts  me.get.ts
│       ├── deactivate.post.ts  export.get.ts
│       ├── verify-email.get.ts  send-verification-email.post.ts
│       ├── forget-password.post.ts  reset-password.post.ts
│       ├── reset-password/[token].get.ts
│       ├── companies.post.ts  companies.get.ts  companies.switch.post.ts
│       ├── companies.update.post.ts  companies.delete.post.ts
│       ├── companies/members.delete.post.ts

│   ├── auth.ts                                # usa identity + audit

├── tasks/

├── error-handler.ts
└── utils/
    ├── apply-custom-sql.ts                      # infra (migrações RLS)
    ├── db.ts                                    # conexão (infra)
    ├── test-setup.ts                            # infra (setup de testes)
    ├── schema/
    │   ├── index.ts                             # registry: re-exporta tudo
    │   ├── identity.ts                          # user, session, account, verification
    │   ├── company.ts                           # company, company_member
    │   ├── invite.ts                            # invite
    │   └── security.ts                          # auth_security_state
    ├── shared/                                  # infra + cross-feature do servidor
    │   ├── audit.ts                             # createAuthEvent (auth_event)
    │   ├── auth-context.ts                      # tipos de event.context (h3 augmentation)
    │   ├── background.ts                        # fire-and-forget com waitUntil (FR-023)
    │   ├── csrf.ts                              # double-submit + isCsrfExempt
    │   ├── email.ts                             # fila Resend (queueEmail)
    │   ├── errors.ts                            # sendXxxError + normalize
    │   ├── hibp.ts                              # breach check (k-anonymity)
    │   ├── ip.ts                                # getClientIp (TRUST_PROXY)
    │   ├── purge.ts                             # corpo compartilhado das purge tasks
    │   └── security.ts                          # rate limit + lockout + CNPJ (auth_security_state)
    └── auth/                                    # ← VERTICAL SLICES
        ├── tenant-context.ts                    # withTenant (infra de tenant, usado pelas features)
        ├── identity/                            # FEATURE: conta + sessão
        │   ├── repository.ts                    # IdentityRepository + identityRepository
        │   └── repository.test.ts               # integração (DB real)
        ├── company/                             # FEATURE: tenant/membership
        │   ├── repository.ts
        │   └── repository.test.ts
        └── invite/                              # FEATURE: convites
            ├── repository.ts
            └── repository.test.ts
shared/                                        # infra do app (inalterado)
├── auth.ts
└── logger.ts
```

### 3.2 Mapa feature → endpoints → tabelas → métodos atuais

> Estado real (2026-09-21 — sem interfaces `I*Repository` nem classes
> `Drizzle*`: uma classe concreta + singleton por feature).

| Feature      | Endpoints (handlers)                                                                                                                      | Tabelas                                                              | Métodos atuais                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| ------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| **identity** | register, login, logout, me, deactivate, export, consent/withdraw, verify-email, send-verification-email, forget-password, reset-password | user, session, account, verification, auth_event (leitura p/ export) | register, signIn, sendVerificationEmail, verifyEmail, requestPasswordReset, resetPassword, validateResetToken, findByEmail, findById, createSession, validateSession, terminateSession, terminateUserSessions, updateUser, isEmailTaken, withdrawConsent, deactivateUser, getUserSessions, listUserAuthEvents (19) |
| **company**  | companies.\*, companies/members.delete                                                                                                    | company, company_member, session (activeCompanyId)                   | createCompany, createCompanyWithOwner, createCompanyMember, searchCompanies, findCompanyById, findUserCompanies, isCompanyMember, getCompanyMembershipRole, removeCompanyMember, setActiveCompany, getActiveCompany, softDeleteCompany, updateCompany (13)                                                         |
| **invite**   | invite, invites.get, invites.accept                                                                                                       | invite                                                               | createInvite, findPendingInvite, listCompanyInvites, acceptInvite (4)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| **security** | (usado por register/login)                                                                                                                | auth_security_state                                                  | checkIpRateLimit, checkEmailRateLimit, checkUserRateLimit, checkAccountLockout, recordFailedAttempt, resetFailedAttempts (+ puras: evaluateRateLimit, evaluateLockout)                                                                                                                                             |
| **audit**    | (usado por middleware + handlers)                                                                                                         | auth_event                                                           | createAuthEvent (+ `background()` p/ fire-and-forget com `waitUntil`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |

> Nota sobre a regra 3 (§3.3, >10 métodos): `identity` (19) e `company` (13)
> excedem o teto. Justificativa registrada: a divisão por subdomínio
> (identidade/sessão/consentimento vs. tenant/membership) já foi feita; fatiar
> mais (ex.: `session/` separado) fica deferido até um segundo contexto
> (`fiscal`, `financeiro`) forçar o padrão.

### 3.3 Regras de ouro (convenções a registrar na constituição)

1. Handler (`server/api/auth/*`) importa **apenas** os repositórios/helpers
   da feature que serve — nunca o repositório de outra feature.
2. Feature **nunca importa** o repositório de outra feature. Se precisar,
   é sinal de fronteira errada → revisar a fronteira.
3. Repositório com >10 métodos deve ser justificado ou dividido.
4. `schema/index.ts` é o único arquivo importado por `drizzle.config.ts`
   e pelo adapter do Better Auth.
5. Toda feature nova segue o template: `repository.ts` + `dto.ts` +
   testes co-localizados; helpers só quando o handler crescer.
6. Cross-feature (rate limit, tenant, erro) vive em `server/utils/shared/`.

---

## 4. Plano de Migração

**Progresso real** (2026-08-09): Fases 1–3 e a parte de middleware da Fase 4
concluídas. Desvios do plano: sem `dto.ts` por feature (os DTOs ficaram no
próprio `repository.ts`); sem interfaces `I*Repository` nem classes
`Drizzle*` (classe concreta + singleton por feature); `db.ts`,
`tenant-context.ts`, `apply-custom-sql.ts`
e `test-setup.ts` permaneceram em `server/utils/` (não movidos para
`shared/`); `security` e `audit` foram para `server/utils/shared/` (D3).
Pendente: Fase 5 (verificação final) e Fase 6 (governança).

### Fase 0 — Fundação (sem mudança de comportamento)

- Criar `server/utils/shared/` e mover: `db.ts`, `tenant-context.ts`,
  `apply-custom-sql.ts`, `test-setup.ts`.
- Mover helpers de erro HTTP (`sendXxxError`, `classifyDatabaseError`,
  `handleDatabaseError`, `withTimeout`, `TimeoutError`) para
  `server/utils/shared/errors.ts`.
- Atualizar todos os imports (`~~/server/utils/auth/helpers` → caminhos
  novos). Rodar `pnpm test` + `pnpm lint` + `pnpm build` — verde antes de
  prosseguir.

### Fase 1 — Dividir schema

- Criar `server/utils/schema/{identity,company,invite,security}.ts` com as
  tabelas de cada feature (mover literalmente, sem renomear colunas).
- Criar `server/utils/schema/index.ts` re-exportando tudo (mesmo formato
  do `* as schema` atual).
- Atualizar `drizzle.config.ts` → `./server/utils/schema/index.ts`.
- Gerar migration vazia para validar que o registro continua íntegro
  (`drizzle-kit generate` sem diff → confirmar com `drizzle-kit check`).
- Rodar testes de integração (RLS continua valendo).

### Fase 2 — Dividir o god repository

- Criar `auth/identity/repository.ts` (19 métodos), `auth/company/repository.ts`
  (13), `auth/invite/repository.ts` (4) — copiando implementações atuais.
- Mover DTOs para `dto.ts` de cada feature.
- Extrair `security/security.ts` (rate limit + lockout) e `audit/audit.ts`
  (createAuthEvent + emitSessionEvent) dos helpers.
- **Bridge temporária**: `server/utils/auth/repository.ts` passa a re-exportar
  as features (`export const authRepository = { ...identityRepository,
...companyRepository, ...inviteRepository }` com o mesmo tipo) para não
  quebrar os handlers em um único commit.

### Fase 3 — Migrar handlers feature a feature

Para cada handler (ordem sugerida: invite → company → identity):

1. Trocar `authRepository` pelo repositório da feature.
2. Ajustar o `.spec.ts` (mock do novo repositório, sem stubs de métodos de
   outras features — bônus do ISP).
3. Rodar testes do endpoint.
4. Remover do bridge os métodos já migrados.

Quando nenhum handler usar mais o bridge: **deletar** `repository.ts`.

### Fase 4 — Middleware, tasks e consolidação

- `server/middleware/auth.ts`: usar `identity` (validateSession via Better
  Auth continua) + `audit` (emitSessionEvent).
- `server/middleware/pii-audit.ts`: usar `audit`.
- `server/tasks/purge-sessions.ts`: usar `identity`.
- Atualizar `nuxt.config.ts` (`nitro.imports.dirs`).

### Fase 5 — Verificação final

- `pnpm test` (unit + integração), `pnpm lint`, `pnpm build`.
- `pnpm format:check`.
- Revisar imports: nenhum import cruzado entre features
  (`auth/identity` não importa `auth/company`, etc.) — checável com
  grep/eslint rule custom (ver Riscos).

### Fase 6 — Documentação & governança

- **Constituição (§II)**: substituir o modelo "repository por contexto"
  pelo modelo "feature = diretório vertical"; adicionar regras 1–6 do §3.3.
- **AGENTS.md**: apontar para este documento.
- **`specs/001-auth-context/plan.md`**: atualizar a seção "Project Structure".
- Atualizar `checklists/architecture.md` (CHK017–019).

---

## 5. O que NÃO muda

- URLs das rotas (`/api/auth/...` intactas — handlers flat).
- Contrato de erros HTTP (`{ error, message }`) e `error-handler.ts`.
- `withTenant` + RLS (defesa em profundidade).
- Co-locação e convenção de nomes de testes.
- Padrão handler fino → repositório → resposta.
- `shared/auth.ts` e `shared/logger.ts`.

---

## 6. Decisões em aberto (confirmar antes da Fase 2)

| #   | Decisão                                                                              | Resolução                                                                                 |
| --- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| D1  | Nome dos diretórios de feature                                                       | **Resolvido**: `identity` / `company` / `invite` sob `server/utils/auth/`                 |
| D2  | Schema: registry único vs glob                                                       | **Resolvido**: registry único em `schema/index.ts`                                        |
| D3  | `auth_event` vira feature própria ou vai para `shared/`?                             | **Resolvido**: `shared/audit.ts` e `shared/security.ts` — cross-feature (regra 6 do §3.3) |
| D4  | Nível de rigidez: quanto os contextos futuros (`fiscal`, `financeiro`) devem adotar? | Em aberto — recomenda-se: obrigatório para código novo                                    |
| D5  | Nome do doc/feature no Speckit                                                       | **Resolvido**: `002-vertical-slices`                                                      |

---

## 7. Riscos & Mitigação

| Risco                                                | Prob. | Mitigação                                                                                                                          |
| ---------------------------------------------------- | ----- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Mudança grande em 1 commit quebra tudo               | Média | Bridge temporário (Fase 2) + migração por endpoint (Fase 3) + CI verde a cada fase                                                 |
| Import cruzado entre features volta a aparecer       | Média | Regra de lint (eslint `no-restricted-imports` para `server/utils/auth/{outra-feature}`) + revisão de PR                            |
| RLS/tenant quebra durante refactor                   | Baixa | Nenhuma query é reescrita — só movida; testes de integração (`repository.test.ts`) validam comTenant/RLS                           |
| Drizzle registry com re-exports perde tipagem        | Baixa | `export * from './identity'` preserva tipos; validar com `drizzle-kit check` + build                                               |
| Custo alto sem valor visível (handlers já são finos) | Média | O valor é no **núcleo** (repositórios/helpers/schema) e na **escalabilidade** — sem a divisão, `fiscal` replicará o god repository |

---

## 8. Resultado Esperado

- `server/utils/auth/` deixa de ter arquivo >300 linhas; cada feature
  fica com <200 linhas de núcleo + DTOs.
- Mocks de teste por feature: cada handler mocka só o que usa (ISP).
- Novo contexto (`fiscal`, `financeiro`) nasce já no padrão vertical.
- Migração reversível por fase — cada fase termina com testes verdes.

# Order API Contracts

Base path: `/api/v1/orders`

Versionamento: `/api/v1` desde já (ADR-005).

Erro padrão: `{ "error": "snake_case_code", "message": "Human-readable message", "details?": {} }`

Rate limits: IP 20/min, email 5/min, user 60/min (sliding window). 429 com `Retry-After`.

CSRF: todos POST/PATCH/DELETE exigem `x-csrf-token` header.

Idempotency: `Idempotency-Key` header obrigatório em POST /api/v1/orders.

---

## POST /api/v1/orders

Cria uma nova ordem.

**Auth**: Required (session cookie + CSRF)

**Headers**: `Idempotency-Key` (UUID v7)

**Request Body**:

```json
{
  "order_type": "dine_in",
  "location_id": "uuid",
  "customer_id": "uuid",
  "notes": "Mesa 5, sem glúten",
  "items": [
    { "product_id": "uuid", "quantity": 2, "unit_price": 29.9, "discount_applied": 0 },
    { "product_id": "uuid", "quantity": 1, "unit_price": 15.0, "discount_applied": 2.0 }
  ]
}
```

`unit_price` no body é **referência apenas** — servidor usa price_list vigente e snapshot no item. Cliente NÃO envia `total_amount`.

**Success Response**: `201 Created`

```json
{
  "id": "uuid",
  "order_type": "dine_in",
  "status": "open",
  "total_amount": "74.80",
  "items": [
    {
      "id": "uuid",
      "product_name": "Pizza Margherita",
      "unit_price": "29.90",
      "quantity": 2,
      "line_total": "59.80"
    },
    {
      "id": "uuid",
      "product_name": "Refrigerante",
      "unit_price": "15.00",
      "quantity": 1,
      "line_total": "13.00"
    }
  ],
  "created_at": "2026-09-22T10:00:00Z"
}
```

**Error Responses**:

| Status | Error Code          | Condition                                                               |
| ------ | ------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 400    | validation_error    | order_type inválido, location_id obrigatório, items vazio, quantity ≤ 0 |
| 401    | unauthorized        | Sem sessão                                                              |
| 403    | csrf_invalid        | CSRF token ausente/inválido                                             |
| 409    | conflict            | Idempotency-Key já processada (retorna resposta original)               |
| 422    | invalid_transition  | N/A na criação                                                          |
| 429    | rate_limit_exceeded | Rate limit excedido                                                     |
| 503    | service_unavailable | DB indisponível                                                         |

---

## GET /api/v1/orders

Lista ordens com paginação cursor-based.

**Auth**: Required (session cookie)

**Query Parameters**: `cursor` (opaque), `limit` (default 50, max 100), `status` (filter), `order_type` (filter), `customer_id` (filter)

**Success Response**: `200 OK`

```json
{
  "orders": [
    {
      "id": "uuid",
      "order_type": "dine_in",
      "status": "completed",
      "total_amount": "74.80",
      "customer_id": "uuid",
      "created_at": "..."
    },
    {
      "id": "uuid",
      "order_type": "retail_pos",
      "status": "open",
      "total_amount": "150.00",
      "customer_id": null,
      "created_at": "..."
    }
  ],
  "next_cursor": "opaque_cursor_or_null"
}
```

**Error Responses**: 401, 429, 503

---

## GET /api/v1/orders/:id

Detalha uma ordem com itens e satélite.

**Auth**: Required (session cookie)

**Success Response**: `200 OK`

```json
{
  "id": "uuid",
  "order_type": "dine_in",
  "status": "preparing",
  "location_id": "uuid",
  "customer_id": "uuid",
  "total_amount": "74.80",
  "notes": "Mesa 5, sem glúten",
  "items": [
    { "id": "uuid", "product_id": "uuid", "product_name": "Pizza Margherita", "unit_price": "29.90", "quantity": 2, "tax_snapshot": {...}, "discount_applied": 0, "line_total": "59.80" },
    { "id": "uuid", "product_name": "Refrigerante", "unit_price": "15.00", "quantity": 1, "discount_applied": 2.00, "line_total": "13.00" }
  ],
  "dine_in": { "table_id": "uuid", "cover_count": 2, "waiter_id": "uuid", "opened_at": "..." },
  "created_at": "...",
  "updated_at": "..."
}
```

**Error Responses**: 401, 404 (fora do tenant = 404 genérico), 429, 503

---

## PATCH /api/v1/orders/:id/status

Transição de estado (FSM por order_type).

**Auth**: Required (session cookie + CSRF)

**Request Body**:

```json
{ "status": "confirmed" }
```

Valida transição permitida pelo FSM do `order_type`. Transição inválida → 422.

**Success Response**: `200 OK` — ordem atualizada

**Error Responses**:

| Status | Error Code          | Condition                                       |
| ------ | ------------------- | ----------------------------------------------- |
| 400    | validation_error    | status ausente ou inválido                      |
| 401    | unauthorized        | Sem sessão                                      |
| 403    | csrf_invalid        | CSRF token                                      |
| 404    | not_found           | Ordem não encontrada (ou fora do tenant)        |
| 422    | invalid_transition  | Transição não permitida (ex.: open → completed) |
| 429    | rate_limit_exceeded | Rate limit                                      |
| 503    | service_unavailable | DB indisponível                                 |

# Data Model: Core Order

## Tabelas

### order

| Campo        | Tipo          | Constraints                                                                              |
| ------------ | ------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| id           | uuid          | PK, default `gen_random_uuid()`                                                          |
| company_id   | uuid          | NOT NULL, FK → company.id                                                                |
| location_id  | uuid          | NOT NULL, FK → location.id                                                               |
| order_type   | text          | NOT NULL, CHECK IN ('dine_in','takeout','delivery','retail_pos','wholesale','quotation') |
| status       | text          | NOT NULL, DEFAULT 'open'                                                                 |
| customer_id  | uuid          | NULLABLE, FK → party.id                                                                  |
| party_id     | uuid          | NULLABLE, FK → party.id                                                                  |
| total_amount | numeric(12,2) | NOT NULL, DEFAULT 0                                                                      |
| currency     | text          | NOT NULL, DEFAULT 'BRL'                                                                  |
| notes        | text          | NULLABLE                                                                                 |
| created_by   | uuid          | NOT NULL, FK → user.id                                                                   |
| created_at   | timestamptz   | NOT NULL, DEFAULT now()                                                                  |
| updated_at   | timestamptz   | NOT NULL, DEFAULT now()                                                                  |
| closed_at    | timestamptz   | NULLABLE                                                                                 |

**Indexes**: `(company_id, location_id, created_at DESC)`, `(company_id, status)`, `(customer_id)`

**Unique**: — (idempotency via tabela separada)

### order_item

| Campo            | Tipo          | Constraints                                                   |
| ---------------- | ------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------- |
| id               | uuid          | PK                                                            |
| order_id         | uuid          | NOT NULL, FK → order.id ON DELETE CASCADE                     |
| product_id       | uuid          | NULLABLE, FK → product.id                                     |
| product_name     | text          | NOT NULL                                                      |
| unit_price       | numeric(12,2) | NOT NULL                                                      |
| quantity         | numeric(12,3) | NOT NULL                                                      |
| tax_snapshot     | jsonb         | NOT NULL                                                      |
| bom_snapshot     | jsonb         | NULLABLE                                                      |
| discount_applied | numeric(12,2) | DEFAULT 0                                                     |
| line_total       | numeric(12,2) | GENERATED ALWAYS AS (round(unit_price \* quantity, 2)) STORED |
| sort_order       | integer       | DEFAULT 0                                                     |
| created_at       | timestamptz   | DEFAULT now()                                                 |

**Indexes**: `(order_id, sort_order)`

### order_dine_in

| Campo       | Tipo        | Constraints                        |
| ----------- | ----------- | ---------------------------------- |
| order_id    | uuid        | PK, FK → order.id                  |
| table_id    | uuid        | NOT NULL, FK → restaurant_table.id |
| cover_count | integer     | DEFAULT 1                          |
| waiter_id   | uuid        | NULLABLE, FK → user.id             |
| opened_at   | timestamptz | NOT NULL, DEFAULT now()            |

### order_takeout

| Campo        | Tipo        | Constraints       |
| ------------ | ----------- | ----------------- |
| order_id     | uuid        | PK, FK → order.id |
| pickup_at    | timestamptz | NULLABLE          |
| pickup_name  | text        | NULLABLE          |
| pickup_phone | text        | NULLABLE          |

### order_delivery

| Campo        | Tipo          | Constraints               |
| ------------ | ------------- | ------------------------- |
| order_id     | uuid          | PK, FK → order.id         |
| address_id   | uuid          | NOT NULL, FK → address.id |
| delivery_fee | numeric(12,2) | DEFAULT 0                 |
| courier_id   | uuid          | NULLABLE, FK → user.id    |
| eta          | timestamptz   | NULLABLE                  |

### order_retail_pos

| Campo          | Tipo | Constraints                    |
| -------------- | ---- | ------------------------------ |
| order_id       | uuid | PK, FK → order.id              |
| register_id    | uuid | NOT NULL, FK → pos_register.id |
| cashier_id     | uuid | NOT NULL, FK → user.id         |
| payment_method | text | NOT NULL                       |

### order_wholesale

| Campo               | Tipo | Constraints               |
| ------------------- | ---- | ------------------------- |
| order_id            | uuid | PK, FK → order.id         |
| po_number           | text | NULLABLE                  |
| payment_terms       | text | NULLABLE                  |
| shipping_address_id | uuid | NULLABLE, FK → address.id |

### order_quotation

| Campo       | Tipo        | Constraints            |
| ----------- | ----------- | ---------------------- |
| order_id    | uuid        | PK, FK → order.id      |
| valid_until | timestamptz | NOT NULL               |
| approved_by | uuid        | NULLABLE, FK → user.id |
| approved_at | timestamptz | NULLABLE               |

## RLS

Todas as tabelas operacionais herdam `company_id` + `location_id` do `order` via JOIN na policy, ou carregam as colunas diretamente (preferível para performance).

Ver `specs/000-foundation/rls-pattern.md` para template normativo.

# Feature Specification: Core Order (Núcleo Unificado)

**Status**: Spec pronta, implementação não iniciada

## Entidade `order`

### Campos Comuns (todas as ordens)

| Campo        | Tipo          | Constraints                                                                              |
| ------------ | ------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| id           | uuid          | PK, default `gen_random_uuid()`                                                          |
| company_id   | uuid          | NOT NULL, FK → company.id                                                                |
| location_id  | uuid          | NOT NULL, FK → location.id                                                               |
| order_type   | text          | NOT NULL, CHECK IN ('dine_in','takeout','delivery','retail_pos','wholesale','quotation') |
| status       | text          | NOT NULL, DEFAULT 'open' — FSM por order_type                                            |
| customer_id  | uuid          | NULLABLE, FK → party.id                                                                  |
| party_id     | uuid          | NULLABLE, FK → party.id (cliente ou fornecedor)                                          |
| total_amount | numeric(12,2) | NOT NULL, DEFAULT 0 — soma das linhas (servidor)                                         |
| currency     | text          | NOT NULL, DEFAULT 'BRL'                                                                  |
| notes        | text          | NULLABLE                                                                                 |
| created_by   | uuid          | NOT NULL, FK → user.id                                                                   |
| created_at   | timestamptz   | NOT NULL, DEFAULT now()                                                                  |
| updated_at   | timestamptz   | NOT NULL, DEFAULT now()                                                                  |
| closed_at    | timestamptz   | NULLABLE                                                                                 |

### Satélites por `order_type`

| order_type | Tabela Satélite  | Campos Extra                                  |
| ---------- | ---------------- | --------------------------------------------- |
| dine_in    | order_dine_in    | table_id, cover_count, waiter_id, opened_at   |
| takeout    | order_takeout    | pickup_at, pickup_name, pickup_phone          |
| delivery   | order_delivery   | address_id, delivery_fee, courier_id, eta     |
| retail_pos | order_retail_pos | register_id, cashier_id, payment_method       |
| wholesale  | order_wholesale  | po_number, payment_terms, shipping_address_id |
| quotation  | order_quotation  | valid_until, approved_by, approved_at         |

### FSM por `order_type` (Mermaid)

```mermaid
stateDiagram-v2
    [*] --> open
    open --> confirmed: confirm()
    open --> cancelled: cancel()
    confirmed --> preparing: start_prep()
    confirmed --> cancelled: cancel()
    preparing --> ready: finish_prep()
    preparing --> cancelled: cancel()
    ready --> completed: close()
    ready --> cancelled: cancel()
    completed --> [*]
    cancelled --> [*]
```

**Transições inválidas = 422** com `{ "error": "invalid_transition", "message": "Cannot transition from X to Y", "details": { "current": "X", "target": "Y" } }`.

### RLS

- `company_id` + `location_id` obrigatórios (ver `specs/000-foundation/rls-pattern.md`)
- Policy: `company_id = get_tenant_id() AND (location_id = get_location_id() OR get_location_id() IS NULL)`

### Idempotency

Criação de `order` **exige** header `Idempotency-Key` (ver `specs/000-foundation/idempotency.md`).

---

## Tabela `order_item`

| Campo            | Tipo          | Constraints                                                   |
| ---------------- | ------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------- |
| id               | uuid          | PK                                                            |
| order_id         | uuid          | NOT NULL, FK → order.id                                       |
| product_id       | uuid          | NULLABLE, FK → product.id                                     |
| product_name     | text          | NOT NULL — snapshot                                           |
| unit_price       | numeric(12,2) | NOT NULL — snapshot                                           |
| quantity         | numeric(12,3) | NOT NULL                                                      |
| tax_snapshot     | jsonb         | NOT NULL — alíquota vigente                                   |
| bom_snapshot     | jsonb         | NULLABLE — ficha técnica                                      |
| discount_applied | numeric(12,2) | DEFAULT 0 — snapshot                                          |
| line_total       | numeric(12,2) | GENERATED ALWAYS AS (round(unit_price \* quantity, 2)) STORED |
| sort_order       | integer       | DEFAULT 0                                                     |
| created_at       | timestamptz   | DEFAULT now()                                                 |

**Snapshot rule** (ver `specs/000-foundation/snapshot.md`): editar produto não reescreve o passado.

---

Ver também: `specs/007-print/` (impressão operacional).

specs/007-print/plan.md: Unicode text, UTF-8 text

# Implementation Plan: Print (MVP)

**Status**: Pronto para implementar
**Base**: `specs/007-print/spec.md` (decisões fechadas)

---

## Project Structure

```
server/
├── api/v1/print/
│   ├── jobs.post.ts           # Enfileira job (idempotente)
│   ├── jobs/[id].get.ts       # Status do job
│   ├── jobs.get.ts            # Lista fila (query: status, printerId)
│   └── jobs/[id]/retry.post.ts # Reimpressão manual
├── tasks/
│   └── process-print-queue.ts # Worker cron (a cada 30s)
└── utils/print/
    ├── templates/
    │   ├── restaurant-kitchen-ticket.ts
    │   └── restaurant-table-comanda.ts
    ├── drivers/
    │   ├── escpos.ts          # Monta bytes ESC/POS text mode
    │   ├── tcp.ts             # Envia via net.Socket
    │   └── index.ts           # Factory por printer.driver
    └── queue.ts               # Helpers de status/retry
```

---

## Tasks (ordem de execução)

### Fase 1: Schema + Migration

- [ ] T001 Drizzle schema `printer` + `print_job` em `server/utils/schema/print.ts`
- [ ] T002 `drizzle-kit generate --name add-print-tables`
- [ ] T003 `drizzle-kit migrate` + validar RLS policies (location-scoped)

### Fase 2: Templates ESC/POS (puro TS, testável unitário)

- [ ] T004 `escpos.ts` — primitivas: `init()`, `text()`, `cut()`, `drawer()`, `feed()`, `encode()`
- [ ] T005 `restaurant-kitchen-ticket.ts` — input: `{orderItem, station, quantity}`, output: string ESC/POS
- [ ] T006 `restaurant-table-comanda.ts` — input: `{order, items, company, pixQR?}`, output: string ESC/POS
- [ ] T007 Testes unitários: snapshots dos bytes gerados (`.spec.ts`)

### Fase 3: Drivers de Transporte

- [ ] T008 `tcp.ts` — `send(host, port, bytes)` com timeout 5s + retry 1x
- [ ] T009 `usb.ts` / `serial.ts` — stub (opcional, só se houver hw)
- [ ] T010 `drivers/index.ts` — `getDriver(printer)` retorna `{send: (bytes) => Promise<void>}`

### Fase 4: API Endpoints

- [ ] T011 `jobs.post.ts` — valida `Idempotency-Key`, cria `print_job` com `content` (base64), retorna `{id, status: 'queued'}`
- [ ] T012 `jobs/[id].get.ts` — retorna job + status
- [ ] T013 `jobs.get.ts` — paginação cursor, filtros `status`, `printerId`
- [ ] T014 `jobs/[id]/retry.post.ts` — reseta `attempts=0`, `status=queued`

### Fase 5: Worker (Nitro Task)

- [ ] T015 `process-print-queue.ts` — cron `0 * * * * *` (a cada 30s)
  - Query: `print_job` where `status='queued' AND attempts < 3` order by `created_at`
  - Para cada: `status='printing'` → `driver.send(bytes)` → sucesso: `printed` + `printed_at`; falha: `attempts++`, `status='failed'` se >= 3
  - Log estruturado: `jobId`, `printerId`, `attempt`, `durationMs`, `error?`
- [ ] T016 Teste de integração: mock driver, validar transições de status

### Fase 6: Integração Restaurant Pack

- [ ] T017 Hook no `kitchen_ticket` creation → auto-enqueue `print_job` (station → printer via `station_ids`)
- [ ] T018 Hook no `order` status `completed` → enqueue `table-comanda` (opcional, behind feature flag)
- [ ] T019 Botão "Reimprimir" no frontend KDS/chama `retry.post`

### Fase 7: Observabilidade + Hardening

- [ ] T020 Métricas Prometheus: `print_jobs_queued`, `print_jobs_printed_total`, `print_jobs_failed_total`, `print_duration_seconds`
- [ ] T021 Alert: `print_jobs_failed_total > 10 in 5min` → ops
- [ ] T022 Documentar `print_job.content` schema (snapshot) em `data-model.md`

---

## Decisões Técnicas (já tomadas)

| Item                  | Decisão                                                                       |
| --------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| ESC/POS library       | **Não usar lib** — `escpos.ts` próprio (50 linhas, text mode only, zero deps) |
| Queue persistence     | `print_job` table (já existe) — sem Redis/BullMQ                              |
| Cron scheduler        | Nitro tasks (built-in) — sem agenda.js                                        |
| Impressora descoberta | IP/porta fixo no cadastro (`printer.connection`) — sem mDNS/bonjour           |
| Idempotency           | Header `Idempotency-Key` (UUID v7) — `specs/000-foundation/idempotency.md`    |

---

## Estimativa (ponytail: 1 dev, sem blockers)

| Fase                     | Dias          |
| ------------------------ | ------------- |
| 1-2 (schema + templates) | 1             |
| 3 (drivers)              | 0.5           |
| 4 (API)                  | 1             |
| 5 (worker)               | 0.5           |
| 6 (integração)           | 1             |
| 7 (obs)                  | 0.5           |
| **Total**                | **~4.5 dias** |

---

## Riscos Conhecidos

- **Impressora offline** → job fica `failed` após 3 tentativas; UX: botão "Reimprimir" resolve
- **Encoding ESC/POS** — testar com modelo real (Epson TM-T20, Elgin i9) antes de merge
- **RLS** — validar que `print_job` respeita `location_id` do worker (worker roda como `merp_app`, seta GUCs via `withTenant`)
